For EACH genus (copy the name exactly) classify spore_class by the genus's diagnostic morphology:
- cryptospore-monad: single spores not in a trilete arrangement and of cryptospore affinity, including hilate monads (e.g. Laevolancis), alete monads and enveloped monads.
- cryptospore-dyad: permanent or dissociated dyads (e.g. Dyadospora, Pseudodyadospora, Segestrespora).
- cryptospore-tetrad: permanent tetrads, naked or enveloped (e.g. Tetrahedraletes, Velatitetras).
- trilete-laevigate: trilete spores with smooth exine, including retusoid forms (e.g. Ambitisporites, Retusotriletes).
- trilete-ornamented: trilete spores with sculpture, such as grana, verrucae, apiculae, muri or ribs (e.g. Emphanisporites, Apiculiretusispora).
- trilete-unspecified: trilete spores in heterogeneous form genera whose species include both smooth and sculptured forms, or whose sculpture is undetermined.
- other-spore: monolete spores, megaspores and other true spores outside these classes; say which in basis.
- not-a-spore: taxa that are not embryophyte spores (acritarchs, algal cysts, misfiled pollen), and names that are not genera ("Spore", "Tetrad", indet.).
- uncertain: a genus of disputed or unknown morphology.

confidence: high if the diagnosis is unambiguous and you know it; medium if inferred; low if unsure.
basis: the diagnostic feature, in a few words.
source: the original description or a standard reference (author and year). Do not invent sources: write "none checked" if you did not verify one.

# silurian_plants: agent guidance

Exploratory, long-term programme on simulation modelling of early land-plant evolution, Late
Ordovician to Early Devonian (c. 458–393 Ma). Read `docs/charter.md` for purpose, stage and
route, and `docs/decisions.md` for what has been settled. Current stage: orientation; no
research question has been chosen, so do not start model code without a framing decision
recorded in `docs/decisions.md`.

## Layout

- `taxa/`: one information card per taxon. `taxa/_template.md` is the schema; `taxa/README.md`
  is generated.
- `docs/`: charter, decisions, primer, precedents review, candidate framings, the source register
  (`docs/sources.md`, the index of all data held and needed: update it when a source changes) and
  the spore-compilation plan.
- `data/timescale.csv`: ICS v2024/12 stage base ages, the single source of numerical ages.
- `data/traits.csv`: generated trait matrix, one row per card.
- `data/eprp/`: material extracted from Barry's EPRP programme: literature, PBDB and Macrostrat
  records, climate and palaeogeography context, and software pointers. Treat it as inputs, not
  verified facts. Read each area's README before use, and regenerate tables with its scripts
  rather than editing them. `data/eprp/local/` is git-ignored (unlicensed, no-derivatives or bulky
  material) and exists only on mac-mini; rebuild it from `deep-server` with the scripts.
- `docs/audits/`: independent checks of cards and deposits.
- `tools/cards.py`: card validation, DOI checking and index generation.

## Commands

```bash
uv run tools/cards.py check   # schema and vocabulary
uv run tools/cards.py dois    # DOIs in taxa/ and docs/ against Crossref
uv run tools/cards.py index   # regenerate taxa/README.md
uv run tools/cards.py matrix  # regenerate data/traits.csv
```

Run `check`, `index` and `matrix` after editing cards, and `dois` after adding references.

## Conventions

- Facts, numbers and citations come from sources read in the task, never from memory. Every
  DOI is verified against Crossref; never guess one. In references lists, one reference per line
  with first author, year, title and DOI, so `dois` can compare them.
- When a source cannot be read in full (paywall, blocked supplement, print only), add it to
  `docs/closed-literature.csv` with what it would settle; Barry obtains these through his
  institutional login. Never bypass access controls. PDFs he obtains go in `library/closed/`,
  which is git-ignored: copyrighted texts are never committed.
- Never send Barry's email address or other personal data to any service or API, including ones
  that ask for a contact email.
- Separate observation, interpretation and speculation; present disputes with both sides.
- Cards record ages as ICS stage names; give finer ages in `range.notes` with their source.
  Literature before 2024 mostly uses the GTS2012 Early Devonian boundaries (Lochkovian 419.2,
  Pragian 410.8, Emsian 407.6 Ma), which differ from ours by up to 3 Myr; see `docs/primer.md` §1.
- Card traits: `absent` means observed absent, `unknown` means not preserved or not reported,
  `na` means not applicable. Add vocabulary to `tools/cards.py` when a real case needs it.
- A card is `draft` until a second agent or Barry has checked its facts; then `reviewed`.
- British spelling. Dense, precise prose; no filler.
- Record decisions that shape the programme in `docs/decisions.md`, naming who decided. Keep
  the AI-roles section of `docs/charter.md` current: the work will be published with a full
  statement of which systems did what.
- Commit to `main` when work reaches a coherent state, and push.

# Charter

Status: **Stage 0, orientation** (opened 2026-10-26). No research question is chosen yet.

## The idea

Barry's starting point, in his words (chat, 2026-10-26): "a simulation modelling project on the
evolution of early land plants (late Ordovician to early Devonian), but it's a very nascent
thought". He frames it as "a long-term development project that might prove infeasible or
uninteresting, but is worth … at least exploring", with no hurry, and with the structure left
free to evolve.

## Why this interval

Between roughly 470 and 393 Ma, land plants went from a dispersed-spore record with no
recognisable bodies, through centimetre-scale leafless axes, to lycopsids and euphyllophytes with
leaves, roots, secondary-growth precursors and metre-scale height. Nearly every innovation that
defines vascular plants appears in that window: desiccation-tolerant spores, cuticle and
stomata, lignified conducting cells, branching sporophytes independent of the gametophyte,
apical dominance, leaves and roots. The same plants are implicated in changes to weathering,
atmospheric CO2 and O2, river form and soil. The record is unusually uneven, with abundant spores
and a few exceptional deposits (the Rhynie Chert above all) set against long gaps in
megafossils, so most explanations of the pattern remain verbal. That combination of a major
evolutionary transition, rich constraints and few quantitative tests is the case for modelling.

## What would make it worth doing

A framing earns a modelling programme if it meets all four tests:

1. **Discriminating evidence.** Competing hypotheses predict outcomes that the fossil, proxy or
   experimental record can tell apart: trait sequences, timing, size distributions, diversity
   or disparity patterns, geochemical signals.
2. **Novelty.** The model adds something the precedents (`docs/precedents.md`) do not: a
   mechanism, a coupling, a confrontation with data, or a reanalysis that changes a conclusion.
3. **Tractability.** The data can be assembled and the model run with resources on the mesh,
   without years of bespoke data collection.
4. **Interest.** Barry finds the question worth his attention.

If no framing passes tests 1 and 2 after orientation, the project is parked with a short written
account of why. That account is itself a useful output.

## Route

0. **Orientation** (current): taxon cards (`taxa/`), a period primer (`docs/primer.md`), a review
   of modelling precedents (`docs/precedents.md`) and candidate framings (`docs/framings.md`).
   Ends with a choice of framing, which is Barry's call on a recommendation from the agents.
1. **Sketch:** the smallest end-to-end model of the chosen framing, deterministic where possible.
2. **Ground:** null models, baselines and the data the model will confront (trait matrices built
   from the cards, occurrence data, proxy series).
3. **Enrich:** one mechanism at a time, each motivated by a discrepancy with data.
4. **Compare:** contrasts that discriminate between competing explanations.
5. **Synthesise:** what is robust, what depends on representation, and what is next.

Stages are a guide, not approval gates. Barry's operating principle applies: reversible work
proceeds and is shown afterwards; first public release, submission and outward-facing messages
are his decisions.

## Outputs

The repository is private for now. Candidate outputs, in any combination: the card collection
and trait matrix as an open dataset, a review or perspective, a modelling paper with code and
data archived, and the human–AI correspondence record published alongside.

## Contributors and AI roles

- Barry W. Brook: originator, scientific adviser and final reviewer.
- Claude Opus 5.5 (Anthropic, `claude-opus-5-5`) in Claude Code: orchestration, programme
  design, synthesis, framings, tooling.
- Claude Sonnet 5.5 (Anthropic) subagents: literature research and first drafts of taxon cards,
  the primer and the precedents review.

`docs/decisions.md` records who decided what and when, for the eventual statement of AI roles.
# Decisions

Dated record of decisions that shape the programme. Newest first. Say who decided, and quote
Barry exactly (with source) or label a paraphrase.

## 2026-10-27: evidence base, tranche 2

- **Scope** (Barry, chat, 2026-10-27): before commenting further on the hypothesis, fill out the
  core evidence base, "to make sure all the relevant data on the time period in question and all
  the specimens (from databases) are properly sourced, indexed and extracted", continuing to work
  with the EPRP session as required.
- **Source register** (Claude): `docs/sources.md` lists every held and missing source with status,
  licence and who does the work. Database and deposit work goes to the EPRP session (request sent
  2026-10-27); literature extraction stays here.
- **Cards, tranche 2.** Barry proposed:
  - the Victorian taxa *Yarravia*, *Hedeia* and *Pluricaulis biformis*; *Dawsonites*; *Palaeostigma*;
  - living comparison taxa *Psilotum*, *Tmesipteris* and *Lycopodium squarrosum*;
  - *Parka*, *Ulothrix* and *Ulva*;
  - group cards on lycopods and trimerophytes; *Archaeopteris*.

  Claude added:
  - *Coleochaete*, *Chara* and the Zygnematophyceae, because the streptophyte relatives are the
    phylogenetically apt life-cycle exemplars; *Ulva* and *Ulothrix* stay as theory exemplars;
  - hornworts and *Marchantia*;
  - group cards for eophytes, rhyniophytoids and zosterophylls;
  - five site cards (Rhynie, Posongchong, Xitun, Bathurst Island, Welsh Borderland mesofossils)
    and a Baragwanathia-flora card, as sampling units for an observation model;
  - the taxa that tranche-1 agents flagged as missing (*Nothia*, *Baragwanathia brevifolioides*,
    *Metzgeriothallus*, *Franhueberia*, *Salopella*);
  - *Wattieza* and *Leclercqia* as anchors beyond the window.

  35 cards in all, drafted by Claude Sonnet 5.5 subagents.
- **Schema** (Claude): card kinds `extant` and `group`, ranks `grade` and `clade`, and groups for
  chlorophyte algae, ferns, progymnosperms and cladoxylopsids. `data/timescale.csv` gains the
  Frasnian, Famennian and Tournaisian bases (ICS v2024/12) for the anchors; the existing rows are
  unchanged.
- **Dispersed spores** (Claude): a scoping study (`docs/spore-compilation-plan.md`) runs in
  parallel with EPRP's check of Palynodata, before any compilation starts.
- **Spore and climate sources found** (EPRP session; reviewed by Claude):
  - Palynodata (GSC Open File 5793, OGL-Canada), with 14,114 window spore rows from 985
    documents, extracted as-is into `data/external/spores/`;
  - HadCM3L land climate (`scotese_02` suite, CC BY 4.0) in `data/external/climate/`.

  Asking Wellman, Steemans and Vecoli for their full 2013 assemblage database is an external
  contact and therefore Barry's decision.
- **Tranche-2 card audit** (two independent Sonnet agents, 8 cards, 99 claims): 80 supported,
  0 wrong, 2 misattributed, 9 imprecise, 9 unverifiable; all fixed
  (`docs/audits/2026-10-27-tranche2-audit-*.md`). The audit confirmed that *Hedeia* is a junior
  synonym of *Yarravia* (McSweeney et al. 2021) and that *Pluricaulis biformis* is an unpublished
  manuscript name. It corrected the *Prototaxites* first-occurrence basis. Both cards stay, with
  their status stated, because they are the names in older literature and in PBDB.
- **Process lapses** (Claude, reported to Barry): a card agent sent Barry's email address to
  Unpaywall's API once; the agent brief now forbids it. A `git add -A` briefly committed the EPRP
  session's unreviewed files; it was reverted within minutes, and only explicit paths are staged
  while another session writes in the tree.

## 2026-10-27: EPRP deposit reviewed and committed

- **Deposit accepted** (Claude, reviewing at Barry's request): `data/eprp/` is committed except
  `local/`, which stays git-ignored. Review: `docs/audits/2026-10-27-eprp-deposit-review.md`.
  All 88 files match their manifest, and the PBDB counts reproduce independently.
- **Facts that changed** (Claude): PBDB holds almost no pre-Emsian spores and only 259 pre-Emsian
  core megafossil occurrences, so F3 needs a dispersed-spore compilation from the literature, and
  its tractability is now moderate. F4's inputs are now on the mesh, so its tractability is
  moderate. Eco-evolutionary vegetation has already been coupled to the carbon cycle for later
  intervals (Rogger et al. 2024, 2026; Gurung et al. 2022), so F5's novelty is moderate.
  `docs/precedents.md` gains an addendum and loses an overstatement; `docs/framings.md` is updated
  to match. The framing choice remains Barry's.

## 2026-10-26: orientation tranche 1 delivered

- **Delivered** (Claude): 24 draft cards, `docs/primer.md`, `docs/precedents.md`,
  `docs/framings.md`, the card index and `data/traits.csv`.
- **Framing recommendation** (Claude, pending Barry's choice): F3 (whether the record supports
  the new timescale, with an observation model) as the first deliverable, and F1 with F2 (the
  rise of the sporophyte) as the core simulation. The precedents review, drafted independently,
  ranked a closed-loop plant–carbon-cycle model (F5) among its top three; `docs/framings.md`
  records the disagreement.
- **Literature that changed the plan.** Hooper et al. (2026, Nature Plants, 22 October) moved
  crown embryophytes to 457–450 Ma and crown tracheophytes to 432–428 Ma, which sharpened F3.
  Loron et al. (2026) argue that Prototaxites was not a fungus. Earp (2024) places the Yea
  Baragwanathia beds mostly in the Early Devonian, which weakens the Silurian-Baragwanathia
  claim. Each is recorded in the relevant card.
- **Card quality** (Claude): a separate Sonnet agent audited a sample of four cards
  (`docs/audits/2026-10-26-card-sample-audit.md`). Of 58 claims, 44 were supported, 0 wrong,
  2 misattributed, 7 overstated and 5 unverifiable. The errors were lost hedges and citation
  drift, not wrong numbers. The nine flagged claims are fixed. All cards stay `draft`: no number
  in them should become a model prior until it has been checked against its primary source.
- **Card conventions settled in use** (Claude): names follow current nomenclature (`Aglaophyton
  majus`, file `aglaophyton-majus.md`); citations use the conventional volume year, and the DOI
  checker accepts the print or online year; DOIs may contain brackets (SICI form).

codex
Some living fern names are valid spore records: Dipteris has monolete spores, while Lycopodium has sculptured trilete spores. Virgatasporites and its synonym Abacum remain disputed in the literature, so I’m leaving their affinity uncertain.
